# TO THE READER. <note>original Latin: "AD LECTOREM"</note>

**Reader,**

<image-desc>A large, square decorative woodcut initial 'W' featuring intricate vine, leaf, and scrollwork motifs.</image-desc>We seek no <term>Maecenas<note>original: "Mecænas." A wealthy, influential patron of the arts or sciences, named after the Roman counselor Gaius Maecenas.</note></term> nor any protection from great persons. For we know that our <term>Philosophy<note>original: "Philofophia." In the context of 17th-century Dutch intellectual life, this often refers to "Natural Philosophy" or Alchemical science.</note></term>, as well as its practitioners, are subject to the contempt of the world: therefore we do not recognize the world in this science. I shall then only establish that the art has no other enemies than those who are ignorant and inexperienced in our Prac- <note>The word continues on the next page as "Practice."</note>

<summary>In this preface, the author asserts that their work does not require the validation of wealthy patrons, acknowledging that true philosophy and its practitioners are often misunderstood or despised by society. He argues that the only critics of this knowledge are those who lack practical experience in the field.</summary>
<keywords>philosophy, patronage, Maecenas, practitioners, science, alchemy, ignorance</keywords>

<margin>Answer.</margin> It is a genuine and universal *Solvent* <term>Menstruum: an alchemical term for a substance that can dissolve solids</term>, called <note>original Latin: "Ignis Aqua"</note> *Fire-Water*, a fiery water and a simple and immortal essence, which is penetrative, bringing all things to their first liquid matter <note>this refers to the alchemical concept of 'prime matter,' the original state of all things</note>. # THE GREAT ARCANUM, <note>original Latin/Dutch: "HET GROTE ARCANUM," meaning the Great Secret or Mystery</note>

power can withstand, for it works without any reaction from the substance being acted upon <note>original: "des Lyders," literally "the sufferer," referring to the material being dissolved</note>; it is unaffected by anything except its own likeness, by which it is subdued: but after it has dissolved all other things, it remains the same, and entirely in its previous nature, and has the same power after a thousand operations as in the beginning.

2 **Question.** What is its substance?
**Answer.** It is a noble, circulated *Salt* <term>Circulated: a process where a liquid is heated so its vapors condense and flow back into the original liquid, often repeated for weeks</term>, prepared by a wondrous art until it fulfills the desire of a diligent artist <note>in this context, the "artist" is the alchemist performing the work</note>; yet it is no bodily *Salt*, made fluid by a simple dissolution, but it is a *Salt-like spirit*, whose heat cannot be hardened by the evaporation of moisture, but is of a *spiritual and essential substance*, volatile with a gentle heat: leaving nothing behind; nevertheless, this spirit is neither *Acid* nor *Alkaline*, but *Salt*.

3 **Question.** What is its likeness?
**Answer.** If you know the one, you shall soon know the other without any

It clarifies that despite its fluid state, it is neither an acid nor an alkali, but a unique, volatile essence created through alchemical circulation.</summary>

### ... or the LIQUOR ALKAHEST.

difficulty. Seek therefore, for the Gods have established the art as a reward for labor.

4 **Question.** What is the immediate matter of the *Alkahest*?

**Answer.** I have told you that it is a *Salt*; the *fire* besieged the *salt*, and the *water* swallowed up the *fire*, but could not overcome it; thus is the fire of the *Philosophers* (of which they speak) made; common things burn by *fire*, and ours by *water*. <note>This refers to "wet combustion" or chemical dissolution, where a liquid acts with the intensity of fire.</note>

5 **Question.** Which is the most noble *Salt*?

**Answer.** If you are eager to learn that, go into yourself, for you carry it with you—both the *Salt* and its *Vulcan* <term>Vulcan: in alchemy, this refers to the transformative fire or the internal heat required to change a substance's state</term>—if only you are capable of distinguishing it.

6 **Question.** What is it? Tell me, I pray you.

**Answer.** Human blood from the body, or its *Urine*, for *Urine* is an excrement, for the most part separated from the blood: each of these two yields a volatile and fixed *Salt*, and if you have the knowledge to gather—

<meta>catchword: gather-</meta>

<summary>This page describes the "immediate matter" of the Alkahest, revealing that it is derived from human biological sources, specifically blood or urine. It explains the alchemical concept of "burning by water" and emphasizes that the necessary components for the Great Work are found within the human body itself.</summary>

--- Page 28 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for preparing the "Balm of Life" from the previous page, finishing the sentence started by the catchword "gather-". # THE GREAT ARCANUM,
to gather and to prepare it, then you shall have the most precious *Balm* of life.

7 **Question.** Is the nature of *Human Urine* more noble than the *Urine* of a *beast*?

**Answer.** There is a great difference: for although it is merely an excrement, nevertheless its *salt* has not its equal in the whole of common nature.

8 **Question.** What are its parts?

**Answer.** A volatile <note>original: "vlugtig"; refers to a substance that easily evaporates or turns into gas</note> and a more fixed one: yet according to the different ways of preparing it, these can be changed in various ways.

9 **Question.** Is there anything in the *Urine* which is distinct from its own *Urinous* nature?

**Answer.** Yes, there is a watery *phlegm* <term>phlegm: in early chemistry, this refers to the tasteless, watery distillate that was considered chemically inactive</term> and *sea-Salt*, which we consume with our food and remains whole and undigested in the *Urine*, and can be separated from it; which (if no appropriate use is made of it in food, after a suitable time) ceases to be present. <note>The author is explaining that common table salt passes through the body unchanged and must be distinguished from the "essential" salts produced by the body itself.</note>

<summary>This page discusses the unique alchemical properties of human urine, distinguishing it from animal urine due to its incomparable salt content. It describes the substance as being composed of volatile and fixed parts, while noting that common sea salt consumed in food remains undigested and distinct from the urine's true nature.</summary>
<keywords>The Great Arcanum, Balm of Life, Human Urine, Alchemical Salt, Volatile, Fixed, Phlegm, Sea-Salt, Digestion</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical inquiry into the composition of human urine, transitioning from the general identification of its parts to the specific origin of its watery "phlegm" and its relation to the Liquor Alchahest. or THE LIQUOR ALCHAHEST. <note>original: "Liquor Alchahest"; in alchemy, this refers to a hypothetical universal solvent capable of reducing any body into its first matter.</note>

10 **Question.** Where does that *phlegm* <term>phlegm: here referring to a tasteless, chemically inactive watery distillate</term> or powerless watery moisture come from?

**Answer.** It is primarily from our drink, and also every thing has its own *phlegm*.

11 **Question.** Explain yourself somewhat more clearly?

**Answer.** You must know that the *Urine* is led to the *bladder* partly through the separating power along with what we drink; partly it consists of a watery *Teffas* <gloss>an expelling humor of the blood</gloss> <note>Teffas is a term used by the alchemist Jan Baptist van Helmont to describe a specific watery waste product of the blood</note> from which being separated—by the scent of the *Urinous Fermentation*—it penetrates very deeply. The *saltiness* remains unchanged, unless the *saltiness* of the blood and *Urine* are both the same, so that everything the *Urine* contains, except the *salt*, is an unprofitable *phlegm*.

12 **Question.** How is it evident that there is an abundant *Phlegm* in the *Urine*?

**Answer.** In this way: first by the *taste*, secondly by the *weight*, and thirdly by the *powers of the same* <note>The original Dutch "fefs" is likely an OCR error for "des zelfs," meaning "of the same" or "of the substance itself."</note>.

13 **Question.** Be your own interpreter? <note>A request for the author to further explain the points just made.</note>

It argues that while the salt in urine is chemically significant, the remaining watery volume is an "unprofitable" substance that can be identified by its lack of taste and weight.</summary>

**Answer.** The *salt* of the *Urine* contains within itself everything that is essentially characteristic of the *Urine*: its *smell* is very sharp, and the *taste* varies according to the different preparations; so that it is also sometimes a *salt* with a *Urine-like saltiness*.

14 **Question.** What have you observed regarding the *weight* of the same?

**Answer.** I have observed this: that about three *ounces* <note>ounce: a unit of weight; in the Dutch apothecary system, roughly 30 grams</note> of *Urine* from a healthy person can easily carry over eighty *grains* <note>grains: a very small unit of weight, approximately 0.06 grams</note> of spring water; from which I have also seen a *Liquor* <term>Liquor: a liquid substance or solution resulting from a chemical process</term> distilled of an equal *weight* to the aforementioned *water*, which made it clear that most of the *salt* had been left behind.

**Answer.** The hardening of the *Urine* by cold <note>likely referring to the formation of ice or the crystallization of residues when chilled</note> is a proof that *Phlegma* is present in it; for the *salt* of the *Urine* does not harden when it is made even slightly moist, even if it were only with water.

21 **Question.** And what further?
**Answer.** In the hardening of the *Urine*, a fine *Spirit of wine* <term>Spirit of wine: "Geest van wijn," usually referring to alcohol (ethanol), but here describing a volatile, flammable spirit extracted from urine that alchemists believed shared the same essence.</term> was discovered.

22 **Question.** Is there such a *spirit* in the *Urine*?
**Answer.** Yes, in truth there is such a one residing in all *Urine*, even from the healthiest *person*, and which can for the most part be prepared by *art* <term>Art: "Konst," referring to the practice of alchemy or "pyrotechnia," the skilled manipulation of substances through fire and chemistry.</term>.

23 **Question.** What activity does this *Spirit* have?
**Answer.** It has such [activity] that it ought to awaken our pity for *mankind*.

24 **Question.** Why is that?
**Answer.** Through this, the *Duelech* <term>Duelech: a term used by the physician-alchemist Paracelsus to describe painful, stone-like deposits (calculi) in the body, such as kidney or bladder stones.</term>, its most heavy enemy, has its beginning.

25 **Question.** Will you give an example of this?
**Answer.** Yes, I will: take *Urine*, and dissolve it in a proper portion of *Saltpeter* <term>Saltpeter: potassium nitrate, a common alchemical reagent used here as a solvent or to facilitate the release of the "spirit" during distillation.</term>, let it stand for a *month*, and then distill it; and there shall come over a *Spirit* that burns on the tongue like a coal of *fire*: repeat this four or five times, drawing off no more than half each time,

31 **Question.** *Does every Spirit of Urine harden every spirit of wine?*
**Answer.** Not at all, for this second *Spirit* lacks that power.

32 **Question.** *What does the Urine contain, being thus prepared, besides the aforementioned Spirits?*
**Answer.** Its more fixed *Urinary Salt*, and by chance, foreign *Sea salt* <note>original: "Zee-fout" — a transcription error for "Zee-sout" (sea salt). In alchemy, "fixed" salts are those that remain stable and do not vaporize easily under heat.</note>.

33 **Question.** *Can this more fixed Salt be brought over the Alembic, with a gentle heat, in the form of a Liquor?*
**Answer.** Yes, that can happen, but here *art* <note>original: "konst" — often referring to "The Art," the specific skillful practice of alchemy.</note> and *labor* are required. <term>Alembic: A classic distillation apparatus consisting of two vessels connected by a tube; the "head" (cap) catches vapors and directs them into a receiver.</term>

34 **Question.** *Where is the phlegm?*
**Answer.** In the *Salt*, for in the preparation or putrefaction, the *Salt* being putrefied in the *phlegm*, rises together with the same. <term>phlegm: In early chemistry, the watery, tasteless liquid that was the first or last part of a distillation, often seen as an "elemental" water.</term>

35 **Question.** *Can they be separated?*
**Answer.** Yes, it is possible, but not by every other *art*.

...when it has been brought to this height?

**Answer.** Examine it, and you shall marvel at what you shall see in the dissolutions of *bodies*. <note>bodies: In alchemy, "bodies" (corpora) refers to solid substances, particularly metals or minerals, which the chemist seeks to dissolve into their primary liquid state.</note>

37 **Question.** *Is this not the Alkahest?* <term>Alkahest: A legendary universal solvent sought by alchemists, capable of reducing any solid matter into its first liquid essence without changing its own nature.</term>

**Answer.** This *Liquor* cannot exist without the communication of the powers of human blood, and the traces of the same are remarkably found in the *Urine*.

38 **Question.** *So then, does the Alkahest lie hidden in the Urine and the blood?*

**Answer.** Nature gives us both blood and *Urine*, and from nature *pyrotechnia* <note>original: "pyrotechnia" — the "art of fire," an early term for chemistry or alchemy that uses heat to transform substances.</note> gives us a *Salt*, which the Art *Circulates* into the *circulated Salt of Paracelsus*. <term>Circulated Salt: A salt that has been refined through a cycle of distillation and condensation (circulation) until it reaches a state of high spiritual potency, a process famously described by the physician-alchemist Paracelsus.</term>

39 **Question.** *You speak very briefly?*

**Answer.** I shall add this to it: the salt of blood ought to be so changed through its *Urinous Fermentation* that it loses its final life, preserves its middle life, and retains its saltiness. <note>Final/Middle Life: Alchemical theory often divided the "vitality" of a substance into stages. Losing the "final life" means the physical, crude form is destroyed, while the "middle life" refers to the internal spirit or medicinal virtue that remains.</note>

40 **Question.** *To what purpose can this serve?*

**Answer.** To defend the excellence that exists in human blood

remains, let the **salt** <note>original: "fout" — a frequent variation of "zout" (salt) in this manuscript, as noted on the previous page.</note> with **water** be drawn off from it, by a **gentle fire** that causes no **sublimation** <term>Sublimation: The process of heating a substance so it turns directly from a solid into a vapor, then back to a solid; here, the author warns against using too much heat to avoid this.</term>, and let there be as much **water** as half of that **Urine** from which these **Dregs** <term>Dregs (Feces): The solid matter remaining after the liquids have been evaporated or distilled away.</term> were dried; and that which is thoroughly soaked with the **water**, let it be gently poured off, or purified **by deliquescence** <term>By deliquescence (Per deliquium): A method where a salt is allowed to absorb enough moisture from the air to dissolve into a liquid, naturally separating it from impurities.</term>: then filter it through a **glass**, let fresh **water** be poured upon it: and repeat this work, until the **salt** becomes pure. ->The<-

# PRACTICE

->Of The<-

# LIGHTS.

The knowledge of *Alchemy* <note>The ancient art and science concerned with the transformation of matter, particularly the transmutation of base metals into gold and the search for a universal elixir.</note> is certainly and without any falsehood true, and not to be mocked, as many ignorant fools <note>original: "domme Ezels" (stupid donkeys)</note> do. Many people toil to obtain this blessed *Tincture* <note>The 'Universal Tincture' or Philosopher's Stone, a substance believed to have the power to perfect imperfect bodies.</note> who possess neither learning nor any insight into *Philosophy* <note>Here referring to 'Natural Philosophy,' the study of nature and the physical universe that preceded modern science.</note>: which causes great shame and mockery, resulting in the belittling of this Noble science of grace. This knowledge and science is only contained within the natural movements or *Circulations* <note>In alchemy, this refers to the cyclical process of distillation where a liquid is boiled and condensed repeatedly in a closed vessel.</note> and revolutions of the *Heavens* above: just as all natural things in the world

<summary>This page serves as an introduction to 'The Practice of the Lights,' defending the legitimacy of alchemy against skeptics and emphasizing that the craft requires deep philosophical and astronomical knowledge.</summary>

# Immature Mineral Electrum (that is), the First Metal is the Ore of Mercury.

Take some of the best *quicksilver* <note>original: *Argentum vivum*</note>, and distill it from its own *ore*; for this purpose, take some of the best *Hungarian ore*, which contains much *quicksilver* within it: one pound will contain twelve, thirteen, or fourteen ounces of *quicksilver*. Distill this *ore* (having first been pulverized) in a glass *retort* <note>a teardrop-shaped glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling substances</note>, with a receiver, then squeeze the *mercury* several times through a piece of *leather* <note>Squeezing mercury through chamois or soft leather was a common method to filter out solid impurities</note>. If you cannot obtain any *Hungarian mercury ore*, then take Spanish, which is the best after the *Hungarian*: squeeze it several times through a piece of *leather*. If you cannot obtain this Spanish *mercury*, then take some of the best *quicksilver*, for which this is the test: place a small amount of it in a silver *spoon*, and hold it over glowing coals,

### PRÆCIPIOLUM.

...of living <note>original: *vivi*; completing the phrase from the previous page</note> mercury is the *Ore of Præcipiolum* <note>original: *Præcipiolum Minera*; the "little precipitate" is considered the essential core of the metal</note>, and that the *Immature Mineral Electrum* <note>original: *Electrum Minerale Immaturum*; an alchemical term for an unrefined, natural alloy or "raw" state of the metal</note> is the *Ore of Living Mercury*. Likewise, Helmont and Paracelsus mention that when the *Living Silver* <note>original: *Argentum Vivum*; quicksilver</note>, or *Living Mercury*, reaches its *destined places* <note>original: *Loca destinata*; the specific vessels or metallic environments intended for the reaction</note>, the *Living Silver* will then leave behind its *Præcipiolum* in the form of a *coagulated Mercury*, while the *Living Mercury* will remain alive and the same as before.

The *destined places* where the *Living Silver* shall leave the *Seed of Mercury* <note>original: *Semen Mercurii*</note> and the *Præcipiolum* are gold and silver. Gold is the best place by reason of its fixedness and purity, and next to that is silver.

In this *Præcipiolum* lies hidden the entire art of the true *Chemistry*. And if a *Chemist* lacks the knowledge to make or separate this *Præcipiolum* from the *Living Mercury*, then he misses the entire art.

This *Præcipiolum* is the substance from which the *Philosophical Mercury* is made—namely, when it has been brought back to its first matter, which is a *clear water*.

The *Philosophers* <note>In this context, "Philosophers" refers to alchemists.</note> call *quicksilver* <note>original: "Argentum Vivum"</note> their wine, and the *Precipiolum* their *tartar*: and both make the *Philosophers' spirit of philosophical wine* <note>original: "Spiritum vini Philosophicum"; an alchemical solvent, not literal wine.</note>, which they now and then give the name of *the star of Mercury* <note>original: "Astrum Mercurii"</note>, *spirit of Mercury* <note>original: "Spiritus Mercurii"</note>, their Fire, their *mineral salt* <note>original: "Sal Minerale"</note> and *sal ammoniac* <note>original: "Sal Armoniacum"</note>, their horse-dung <note>A term for a gentle, steady heat source often used in digestion processes.</note>, their *water-bath* <note>original: "Balneum Mariæ"</note>, and a hundred other names more, to mislead the people.

# Van HELMONTS

Take it then and put it again into the *Retort*, and distill it as before, discarding no *Powder* which you might think is *Dregs* <note>original: "Fæces"; the inert waste material or sediment left after a chemical process.</note>; for if you discard it, I tell you, you will lose the *Præcipiolum* <note>original: "Præcipiolum"; a term used by the chemist Jan Baptista van Helmont to describe a specific chemical "starter" or precipitate that acts as a catalyst.</note>. The reason is that your *Gold* is the *Loadstone* <note>original: "Zijl-steen" (zeilsteen); a magnet. The author suggests the gold has an "attractive" power over the newly formed substance.</note> which has drawn the *Præcipiolum* to itself, or it is the *Vessel* in which the *Philosophical Wine* has dropped its *Tartar* <note>In winemaking, tartar is the sediment that forms in a barrel; here, it is a metaphor for the valuable chemical precipitate forming within the gold.</note>, which *Paracelsus* and *Helmont* call the *Præcipiolum*.

Now, how shall the *Philosophical Tartar* or *Præcipiolum* be separated from our *Gold*?

### PRÆCIPIOLUM.
->E 3<-

*Gold* is filled with *Præcipiolum*: and then it is time to separate the *Præcipiolum* from the *Gold* and the *Mercury*, which is *women’s work* <note>original: "Vrouwewerk"; a common alchemical metaphor for the stage of cleaning, washing, or gentle heat, likening the laboratory work to the domestic chore of doing laundry.</note>; when their clothes are dirty, they wash the filth away again. And so you must purify your *Præcipiolum* from your *Gold* and *Mercury* in the following manner.

When you have the sign that your *Mercury* will only mix with your *Gold* with great difficulty, and that your *Gold* does not want to enter into your *Living Mercury* <note>original: "Mercurium Vivum"; the standard term for liquid metallic mercury or quicksilver.</note>, then pour upon it some of the clearest and most beautiful *water* (distilled is best) so that your gold is covered by the width of three fingers; wash it well, stirring the *Gold*, the *Mercury*, and the *water* that is in the *mortar* <term>Mortier|A bowl-shaped vessel used for grinding and mixing substances with a pestle.</term> well together with your glass *Pestle* <term>Stamper|A club-shaped tool used for mashing or grinding materials in a mortar.</term>, until you see that your *water* becomes bluish-black. That is the sign that the gold is dropping the *Tartar* <note>original: "Tartarum"; as used previously, this refers to the sediment or precipitate being released from the metal.</note> or *Præcipiolum* into the *water*. Then pour that *water* off into another *glass*, and take great care that no *Mercury* goes into it with the water. Then pour more *fresh water* onto your *Gold* and *Mercury*, and wash it again as before,

<page-num>40</page-num>
<header>VAN HELMONT'S</header>

Note well: if the water should diminish, fresh water should be added to it from time to time.

Now, the sign [that the process is complete] is when the *mercury* has lost all its *seed* <note>original: "Sperma"; in alchemy, this refers to the generative or essential principle of a substance.</note> or its *tartar* <note>original: "Tartar"; usually referring to stony residues or impurities that settle out of a liquid.</note> or its *coagulated mercury* or its *precipiolum*: then the *gold* and the *mercury* will always mix well. Even if you were to distill and wash it a thousand times, the water will always remain clear and not blue. As long as there is any *precipiolum* in the *mercury*, the *gold* and the *mercury* mix with one another only with difficulty; when you wish to achieve a mixture, it must first be washed, and then it will mix readily. So I say, when your *precipiolum* has already been separated from the *mercury*, even if you were to distill your *mercury* from your *gold* a thousand times, they will always mix together very readily and leave no *powder* behind; it will all enter the *mercury*, and your water will remain clear and bright.

And now further to our purpose, to bring the *precipiolum* to a medicine,

# PRECIPIOLUM.

->The dose is two or at most three grains.<- <note>A "grain" was a minute unit of weight, roughly 65 milligrams.</note>

### Composition of the Water of Egg Whites.
<note>original: "Compositio Aquæ Albuminum Ovorum."</note>

Take a quantity of *eggs*, boil them very hard, take the *whites* of the same and cut them very small, and distill them *by ashes* <note>original: "per cineres"; heating the vessel in a container of ash for gentle, even temperature.</note> through an *alembic* <note>An apparatus consisting of a boiling pot and a cap with a tube for collecting condensed vapors.</note> very gently, until you have drawn off all the water from the *whites*: then take the *eggshells*, burn them to *ashes*, and put these in a *retort* <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for high-heat distillation.</note>, and pour their own water back over them, and distill it very strongly *by sand* <note>original: "per Arenam"; a sand bath allowed for much higher temperatures than water or ash baths.</note>: then pour this water back onto the *ashes*, and distill it again

# THE PRECIPIOLUM

<header>THE PRECIPIOLUM.</header>

and increases with the Full *Moon*, and one grain of the same *purges* very gently all *gouty* <term>Podagreuſe; relating to podagra, a painful form of inflammatory arthritis, usually gout</term> poisonous and *dropsical* <note>original: "water-sugtige"; referring to dropsy, an old term for edema or swelling caused by excess fluid.</note> humors, being prepared with grains of your *precipiolum*.

---

->*In what manner the* Precipiolum *is to be brought back to its first or slippery matter.*<-

Take your *salt*, and twice as much of your *Precipiolum* that has been made dry: first *dissolve* your *salt* in clean warm *water*, and pour that onto the *Precipiolum*, and let it gently evaporate in warm *ashes* <note>A common alchemical technique using a bed of warm ashes to provide a steady, indirect heat.</note>, with a little *fire*: then take your *Precipiolum* with its own *salt*, and put it into a small *retort* <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-curving neck used for distillation.</note>, and pinch the neck of the *retort* very tightly, or melt it together, or seal it with the *seal of Hermes* <note>original: "zegel van Hermes"; an airtight, or "hermetic," seal created by melting the glass neck of a vessel closed.</note>: then place it in a *vapor bath* <note>original: "Balneum Vaporofum"; a method of heating where the vessel is suspended over boiling water to be warmed by steam.</note>, and let it stand for six weeks in digestion or putrefaction, and it shall become a slimy *water*: then open the neck of your *retort*, and place it in a *sand furnace* <note>original: "Sant-Forneys"; a furnace where the vessel is buried in sand to ensure even heat distribution.</note>, cover it well with an earthen *pan*, and place a well-*luted* <note>original: "geluteerden"; sealed with "lute," a paste or cement used to make laboratory apparatus airtight.</note>

The reason for this is that *Gold* and *Silver* and all other metals are of the same *nature*, and have their beginning from the same *water*; and there is nothing in the world that does not have its beginning from the same.

### THE GENERAL MEDICINE.

Van HELMONT'S &c.

*Gold is the greatest secret of nature.*

With this *Liquor* <note>a term used in alchemy for a potent liquid substance or essence</note> *Raymond Lullius* <note>a 13th-century philosopher whose name was frequently attributed to later alchemical texts</note> refers to the *Mercury* in *Nature Disemboweled* <note>original Latin: *Exenterata Natura*, a title of a known alchemical treatise</note>.

This writing was given by a *German* to Mr. *Richard Prat*, who lived in *Hampshire* and had cured the *German* of an illness; as a token of friendship, the *German* gave him these papers, assuring him that they were written by *Van Helmont* <note>Jan Baptist van Helmont (1580–1644), a pioneering Flemish physician and chemist</note>: and Mr. *Prat* gave them to Mr. *Jalden*, the Minister of *Compton* in *Surrey*, who is the reason that these have come to light; and this Mr. *Jalden* is a very upright man, and very eager to do all the good that is possible for him in this world: to which his neighbors and the poor of his parish are ready to testify abundantly. *Farewell.* <note>original Latin: *Vale*</note>

### DRINKABLE GOLD <note>original: "AURUM POTABILE."</note>

...put it into a glass *Basin* and grind it with the bottom of another round *glass* until it becomes a *Powder*: then put it into a *glass shaped like a urinal* <note>A common pear-shaped laboratory vessel used by alchemists and physicians.</note>, holding about three *small cups* <note>original: "mutsjens." An old Dutch liquid measure, approximately 150ml.</note>, and pour in a good one-and-a-half *cups* of *Spirit of Wine* <term>Spirit of Wine: high-proof distilled alcohol (ethanol), used here as a solvent to extract the medicinal properties</term>: place this *glass* in a cold spot until it turns red, which will take about 10 days; shake it frequently every day until three days before you wish to pour it off: then gently pour off the pure *Liquid* and place that pure liquid into a *distilling glass* (or another *glass* until you have collected more): then add more *Spirit of Wine* to the *dregs* <note>original: "Fæces."</note>, and proceed as you did with the previous batch; if it is still highly colored, add *Spirit of Wine* for a third time: combine all these colored *Liquors* and distill them until the *residue* (called the *Tincture*) becomes as thick as a *Syrup*.

Take one *Ounce* of this *Tincture* and put it into a pint of *Canary Sack* <note>original: "Canary Seck." A sweet, fortified white wine from the Canary Islands, very popular in the 17th century.</note>, and when it has cleared (which shall take about a day and a half), you may then drink of it.

Ensure you obtain the necessary glasses, including three or four *distilling glasses*, each holding two or three *mengels* <note>An old Dutch liquid measure, roughly equivalent to 1.2 liters.</note>. See that you obtain about 10 *mengels* of red and strong *wine-vinegar* (wine-vinegar made from *Claret* or *French wine* being far too weak); it should be made from red *Wine*, *Sack* <note>A dry, fortified wine, often from the Canary Islands.</note>, or *Muscatel*. ## <term>Philosophical Stone<note>Also known as the Philosopher’s Stone, this refers to the legendary alchemical substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting eternal life.</note></term>.

Considering the long desire and hope of the *students* in the *art* of <term>Chemistry<note>original: "Chimie." Latin for the Moon, representing silver.</note>, or rather *Man* and *Woman*, the *Man* being hot and dry, and the *Woman* cold and moist: and know this for certain, that our *Stone* is not composed of any other things: although many *Philosophers* name many and various other things, of which they speak in a *Sophistical* <note>In alchemical literature, "sophistical" refers to deliberately misleading or coded language intended to hide secrets from the "unworthy" or uninitiated.</note> manner.

And this I have deemed necessary to put forward, concerning the subject of our art.

In alchemy, Sulfur represents the active, solar, masculine principle of combustion and soul.</note>, when it is joined with its *water* or *Mercury* <note>original: "Mercurius." The progress is nothing else than a certain contradictory operation: for the description of contradictory things is one and the same, and if you have made this reconciliation twice, then you shall be able to finish the entire work.

But now the whole art consists in extracting the *Elements*. And therefore read that which follows here so often, until you can grasp and understand it; and know that no one has spoken so openly as I do in this place, as you shall find by what follows; give thanks therefore to that *Great God*, and be not ungrateful to your *Friend* <note>A common alchemical convention where the author addresses the reader as a "friend" or "brother" initiated into the secrets.</note>, who has shared this *Treatise* <note>original: "Traktaat." The *Extraction* of the *Elements* is a certain composition of *blackness*, *whiteness*, *yellowness*, and *redness* <note>These represent the four main stages of the alchemical Great Work: Nigredo (black), Albedo (white), Citrinitas (yellow), and Rubedo (red).</note>: and know that the nature of their root ought to be extracted. # Of the Philosopher's Stone.

<center>G 5</center>

Now there remains the multiplication <note>In alchemy, multiplication is the process of increasing the quantity or the transmuting power of the Philosopher's Stone after its initial creation.</note>, regarding which this is briefly to be noted: that the *Elixir* ought to be fed with the same things from which it first had its composition. No *Philosopher* before me has declared this so openly as I have done here, and that for two reasons: firstly, because from the beginning of the *work* until the end, a long time is required, although some *Philosophers* say that the *Stone* can be made in a day; and others set a Month for it: but know that they speak in parables <note>original: "gelijkenis." Alchemists used allegorical language to hide secrets from the uninitiated; here, "days" or "months" might refer to specific chemical cycles rather than literal time.</note>, and that their words must not be understood in that way.

Nevertheless, I say with *Scotus* <note>Likely Michael Scot (c. 1175–c. 1232), a medieval scholar and mathematician whose name was frequently associated with alchemical manuscripts.</note> that the *Stone*, or the perfect work, can be made in a Year. Secondly, [I speak openly] because man's life is short, and he grows old before he grasps and understands what is necessary to be done in the *Composition* of the *Stone*. And therefore I have here so openly explained all things, out of fear that this so Noble knowledge might be lost and come to nothing.

# Of the Philosopher's Stone.
G 5

Now there remains the *Multiplication* <note>Multiplication is the process in alchemy by which the power and quantity of the transmuted substance—the Elixir—is increased.</note>, concerning which it is briefly to be noted: that the *Elixir* ought to be fed with the same things from which it first had its composition. No *Philosopher* before me has declared this as openly as I have done here, and that for two reasons: firstly, because from the beginning of the *Work* <note>original: "werk" — referring to the *Magnum Opus* or "Great Work" of alchemy.</note> until the end, a long time is required, although some *Philosophers* say that the *Stone* can be made in a day: and others set down a Month for it: but know that they speak in parables <note>original: "by gelijkenis" — meaning allegorically or through metaphors.</note>, and that their words must not be understood in this way.

Nevertheless, I say with *Scotus* <note>Likely Michael Scot (c. 1175–c. 1232), a medieval scholar and polymath who, in alchemical tradition, was credited with several influential texts.</note> that the *Stone*, or the perfect work, can be made in a Year. Secondly, because the life of man is short, and he grows old before he perceives and understands what is necessary to be done in the *Composition* of the *Stone*. And therefore I have here so openly explained all things, for fear that this so Noble knowledge might be lost, and come to nothing.

### The Theory of the same Author.

Use the honorable Nature, for the *Philosophers* <note>In this context, "Philosophers" refers to alchemists—those who seek to understand the secrets of nature.</note> have, by their own authority, attributed many names to this *Nature*, because of the various colors appearing in its transformations; for when it appears in the form of *water*, they have called it *Argentum Vivum* <note>Latin for "Living Silver," the common alchemical name for Mercury.</note>, permanent *Water*, spirit of lead, *Spittle of Luna* <note>In alchemy, "Luna" is the code name for Silver; "spittle" or "saliva" refers to a liquid or distilled essence of that metal.</note>, *Tin*, etc. And when it is made dry and white, they have called it *Silver Magnesia* and white *Sulphur*. And when it is red, they call it *Gold* and *Ferment* <note>A "ferment" in alchemy is a substance that acts like a catalyst or leaven, used to "ripen" the work into its final, perfect state.</note>. But they do not differ in the matter itself, for it is always the same thing, and the same substance, and always of the same *Nature*, into which nothing can enter that has not been drawn from it, and that which is closest to it and of its own *Nature*. This is true: that the *Stone* is one, and one *Medicine*, and it is a clear, bright, and permanent water: pure and shining with a Heavenly

and if no water entered into our Medicine, then it could not purify or improve itself: but that which improves it is *Sol* <note>Latin for "Sun," the alchemical name for Gold.</note>: for the water cannot be improved without it; for no colored poison <note>In alchemical literature, "poison" often refers to the crude, toxic, or "unripe" state of the materials before they have been purified and transformed into the Medicine.</note> can be brought forth without *Sol* and its shadow. Therefore, he who thinks that a *Tincture* <term>Tincture: A potent alchemical substance or "stain" capable of infusing its properties into other metals, specifically to transmute them into gold or silver.</term> can be made without these two bodies, namely *Sol* and *Luna* <note>Latin for "Moon," the alchemical name for Silver.</note>, proceeds in the *Practice* like a blind man: for a body does not act upon a body, nor a *Spirit* <note>A "Spirit" refers to the volatile parts of a substance that can be distilled or sublimated, as opposed to the solid, "fixed" Body.</note> upon a *Spirit*: nor does form receive an impression from form, nor matter from matter, and nothing that is like another acts actively or passively upon each other: for one is no more worthy than the other, and therefore no action can take place between them, since one equal cannot rule over another equal. But a *Body* receives an impression from a *Spirit*, just as matter receives its form, and a *Spirit* from its *Body*, for they are so made and created by *God*, that they [act] upon each other

### *The Theory of the same Author.*

Use Honorable Nature, for the *Philosophers* <note>The "Philosophers" refers to the long lineage of alchemical authorities.</note> have, by their own authority, devised many names for this *Nature* because of the various colors appearing during its transformation; for when it appears in the form of *water*, they have called it *Argentum Vivum* <note>original: "Argentum Vivum," Latin for "Living Silver" or Mercury.</note>, permanent *Water*, spirit of lead, Spittle of *Luna* <note>Latin for "Moon," the alchemical name for Silver; "spittle" refers to a mercurial secretion or distillate.</note>, *Tin*, etc. and when it is made dry and white, they have called it *Silver Magnesia* <term>Magnesia: Not the modern mineral, but a term for the "crude" or "raw" matter of the Stone before it is purified.</term> and white *Sulphur*. And when it is red, they call it *Gold* and *Ferment* <term>Ferment: A substance—usually gold or silver—added to the alchemical base to "leaven" it and give it a specific form, much like yeast in bread-making.</term>. But they do not differ in the thing itself, for it is always the same thing, and the same substance, and always of the same *Nature*, into which nothing can enter that has not been drawn from it, and that which is closest to it and of its *Nature*. This is true: that the *Stone* is one, and one *Medicine*, and it is a clear, bright, and permanent *water*: pure and shining with a Heavenly

BERN. TREVISAN,

consider, who says: mix *Water*, *Earth*, *Air*, and *Fire* together in a proper weight; so you shall, without doubt, obtain all the secrets of these Divine Sciences. And first, when he says: "put the *earth* into the *water*," or "let it rot <note>In alchemy, "rotting" (putrefaction) is the stage where matter breaks down and blackens, which was believed to be necessary for new life or transformation to begin.</note> therein," this means nothing else than the extraction of *water* from *earth*, and the pouring of *water* onto *earth*, until the *earth* is rotted and purified; otherwise it would not bring forth its fruit. Secondly, when he says: "mix *water* with *Air*," that is nothing else than as if he wished to say: mix *water* that is now prepared with dissolved <note>original: "ontbondene"</note> *Air*, or unite and mix dissolved <note>original: "gediffolveerde"</note> *Air* with *water*. ## The Preparation of the Ferment for the White Stone.

Take then *Silver* that is well purified of all other *Metals* and filthiness that it has been joined with, and dissolve the same in as much of our *Lunaria* <term>Lunaria: Literally "moon-plant," here referring to a specific alchemical solvent or "Moon-water" used to dissolve silver.</term>—which is our *Mercury*—as corresponds to the amount of your *Silver*, and above all ensure it is no more; set it upon warm *Ashes* tightly covered, and when it is entirely dissolved, then all the *Liquor* <note>original: "Liquor"; the resulting liquid solution.</note> shall be green: then rectify <term>Rectify: To purify a liquid through repeated distillation.</term> our *Mercury* two or three times cleanly from it, so that nothing of it remains behind; then seal the *Oil of Luna* <note>Oil of Luna: the dissolved, oily essence of silver, the "Moon."</note> in a *Chimia* <note>original: "Chimia"; likely a chemical vessel or a specific type of flask.</note>, and set it in a *Balneum* <term>Balneum: A water bath (Bain-Marie), used for gentle, consistent heating.</term> to putrefy <note>original: "verrotten"; the essential alchemical stage of decomposition and blackening that must occur before the matter can be reborn.</note>, until it displays all colors <note>Alchemists looked for the "Peacock’s Tail," a stage where many colors appear in the vessel, signaling the transition to the final white or red stage.</note>, and finally becomes *Crystalline-white*; which is then the *white Ferment* of *Ferments*.

<summary>This page provides the recipe for creating the "White Ferment," which involves dissolving purified silver in an alchemical solvent, distilling it, and subjecting it to gentle heat until it reaches a pure white state.</summary>

### *The Fermentation of the white Stone.*

From Sir GEORGE RIPLEY.

### A Shortening of the Great Work, which saves half of the effort and labor in the work, having been discovered by Sir George Ripley.

Take the *white frosty rime* <note>original: "witte vorstige Rijm"; referring to a crystalline, frost-like deposit</note> or *powder*, of which I spoke to you at the beginning, being found in the neck of the *retort* <term>Retort</term> <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling substances.</note> after the withdrawal of the *menstruum* <term>Menstruum</term> <note>In alchemy, a solvent capable of dissolving solids; often seen as the "mother" or "mercury" of the work.</note> that is like a sublimate. It is the perfect *sulfur* <term>Sulfur</term> <note>The alchemical principle of the "soul" or "combustibility," not necessarily the chemical element sulfur.</note> of nature, and therefore requires neither rotting <note>original: "verrotting"; also known as putrefaction, the stage where the matter turns black.</note> nor digestion <note>original: "verteeringe"; a period of gentle, steady heat.</note> to become *white*. Take this *sulfur*, either half or all as it pleases you, and also take as much of the *luna ferment* <term>Luna Ferment</term> <note>A catalyst made from silver (Luna) used to "leaven" or direct the alchemical matter toward the creation of silver.</note>—when it is the *ferment of ferments*—as corresponds to the fourth part of the said *sulfur*. Seal them both together in a *chemical vessel* <note>original: "Chimia"; likely a hermetically sealed glass vial.</note>, and fix them in a *fixing-vessel* <term>Fixation</term> <note>The process of making a volatile substance stable so that it no longer evaporates when exposed to heat.</note> under the *fire*, which shall be well completed in three days. And when it has become a fine *white powder*—

<summary>This page introduces an abbreviated method for the alchemical "Great Work" by Sir George Ripley, focusing on using a "white frosty powder" found in a retort. This substance is identified as the "perfect sulfur of nature" and is combined with a silver catalyst (Luna Ferment) to skip the lengthy stages of putrefaction and digestion.</summary>

The Header: The BOSOM-BOOK, <note>A "Bosom-book" refers to a manual or Vade Mecum intended to be carried close to one's person for constant reference.</note>

...is abundant or diminished, teaching how to make infinite *Gold* and *Silver* after the <term>Medicine</term> <note>In alchemy, the "Medicine" refers to the Philosopher's Stone or Elixir, believed to "cure" imperfect metals by turning them into gold, as well as healing the human body.</note> was prepared, also having the power to heal all discomforts and illnesses, whether they arise from cold or hot causes <note>A reference to Galenic humoral theory, where diseases were classified by their "temperature" (e.g., a fever was hot, while phlegmatic conditions were cold).</note>, through the subtlety of fine Nature, above all other Medicines or Doctors; for it refreshes the healthy, strengthens the weak, and makes the old appear young, drives away all discomfort, and casts off the venom from the heart; it moistens the veins and joints, dissolves everything in the Lungs, purifies the Blood, purges the pipes <note>Likely referring to the bronchial tubes or the circulatory vessels.</note>, and keeps them clean: and when the illness has lasted a month, it heals it in a day; if it has lasted a year, it heals it in 12 days: and when the ailment is chronic, it is healed by the same in a month. To conclude: he who possesses this Medicine has an incomparable Medicine, above all the Treasures of the World.

->*Praise the Lord.*<-

# THE MIRROR OF ALCHEMY <note>original: "Speculum Alchymiæ"</note>

# ALCHEMY,

->By **ROGER BACON**.<-

*Very Dear Friend*,

I send to you, along with my greetings, the *mirror of alchemy*, which I have imprinted in my heart and have drawn from the books of wise men; in which is fully contained all that they have gathered for the perfection of *alchemy*: and I give it to you, as to one in whom all is gathered together, that which is scattered among others in various places, and to this <note>The text ends here at the catchword "art" (konst), which would begin the following page.</note>

<summary>This page serves as the title page and introductory epistle for Roger Bacon's "Mirror of Alchemy," where the author claims to have distilled the wisdom of many masters into a single, concise volume for a dear friend.</summary>
<keywords>Roger Bacon, Mirror of Alchemy, Speculum Alchymiæ, Alchemical Art, Hermetic wisdom, perfection of alchemy</keywords>

art is required. I shall answer your great knowledge thus: that all things are created by the four *Elements*, and that these are the roots and substances of all things, and that their distinction consists in three things: namely, *Color*, *Smell*, and *Taste*. In my view, there are only three variations of the *Elements*: namely, various combinations, various decoctions <term>kokingen: the alchemical process of "cooking" or maturing a substance through steady heat, often referred to as digestion</term>, and various mixtures. Therefore, when you wish to transform a Metallic body into another, you must know the *Nature* of each and understand their distinction: and when you know this, then you can—through subtraction and addition—bring together more of one *Element* or of another, and cook them well or poorly together, and so mix them well or poorly, according to your own desire. And every man would be able to do this in all metals, if he knew without fail how he should separate the *Elements*; that is to say, how he should return them to their first root and substance; which roots [are] Sul-fur

<meta>catchword: fur</meta>

<summary>Bacon explains that all matter is composed of the four elements, distinguished by color, smell, and taste. He asserts that transmutation is possible if one understands how to separate and recombine these elements by returning them to their primary state of "Sulfur."</summary>

<keywords>four elements, transmutation, alchemy, decoction, metallic bodies, sulfur, roots of matter, Roger Bacon</keywords>

### ALCHEMY. ###

K 4

many [call] *Quick-silver*, or *Sulphur* and ☿ <note>The alchemical symbol for Mercury, representing the planet and the metal</note>: and then that is the root or the substance most immediate. But because the separation of the *Elements* in *Metals* is heavy and difficult, the Masters—in order to find the roots of the same most easily and closely—made their separation of the *Elements* from *Sulfur* and *Quick-silver*. These they used, and they said that only the *Elements* in the *Metals* were fixed <note>original: *vast*; in alchemy, a "fixed" substance is one that can endure the heat of fire without evaporating or being destroyed</note>; and that foreign *Elements* from other things, such as *blood*, *eggs*, and *hair* <note>A reference to "organic" alchemy, which Roger Bacon and other "mineral" alchemists often criticized as ineffective compared to working with metallic ores</note>, do not enter except by force and the mixing of the same with the aforementioned *Elements*, drawn from the *Metallic spirits* and *bodies*. But because we cannot soften or separate as *Nature* does—for she separates without the addition of any foreign thing over the span of a thousand years, and our lives being not so long—therefore, when we wish to make this separation, we must invent a science through which we can do it more quickly: and this we cannot do unless we [use] things that [are] against—

<summary>Bacon argues that while Nature takes a thousand years to refine metals, alchemists must use a specialized science to achieve the same results quickly, focusing on the "fixed" elements of sulfur and mercury rather than organic additives.</summary>

<keywords>alchemy, sulfur, mercury, quick-silver, four elements, organic alchemy, metallic spirits, Roger Bacon, transmutation, fixing</keywords>

-># SPECULUM #<-

Therefore, when two contradictory things are mixed with one another, the one works upon the other, and they give to each other a portion of their quality and powers: and this is the reason why you must first learn to know the qualities and properties, before you undertake to mix them together in their own *Nature*; and it is necessary that you know the operation of *Nature*, which you intend to imitate, and what and how much each thing gives of its quality and *Nature*, and how much and what it lacks of another’s quality and *Nature*, by means of the work you do therewith, and through the *Nature* of contradictory things which you mix together: and if you err in any of these—namely, in how much and what—(for when you know this, then you can *rectify* <note>original: *rectificeeren*; in alchemy, this means to purify a substance by repeated distillation or refinement until it reaches its most potent state</note> all things in the world,

And *Avicenna* <note>Ibn Sina (c. 980–1037), the Persian polymath whose medical and philosophical texts were central to medieval alchemical thought</note> says the same, and so all your desires ought to be directed toward this: for I have said this to be the principle or the roots of *Philosophical Alchemy* and *Medicine*. # MIRROR # <note>original: *Speculum*; a common title for instructional or encyclopedic texts meant to "reflect" a branch of knowledge</note>

The first [thing] is that you must know how to prepare all your materials <note>original: *dingen* (things/matters)</note> well, and how to expel that which is most hindering to them, and how to obtain that which most agrees with your intention; and that you recognize the sign when you have what you desire, and know how to remove that which must be removed: for everything that man does has an end and a certain determination; as according to the saying of the *Philosophers*, when Nature intends to destroy any thing in order to make another thing come forth—be it better or worse—she seeks a certain degree which she does not pass, but remains standing there, and then through another thing that is prepared, she brings forth another distinct form <term>Form (gedaante)</term><note>In alchemical and Aristotelian philosophy, "form" is the essential nature or shape that makes a substance what it is</note>, which it did not have before.

The second [thing] is that you know how to mix your materials together well when they are prepared; that is, to make one substance <note>original: *selfstandigheyt* (literally 'self-standingness')</note> from various things, which remains inseparable for all time. For if you do not know [how to mix] the things well and naturally...

### OF ALCHEMY <note>Latin: *ALCHYMIÆ*</note>

...and therefore, through a lack of knowledge of the same, great errors arise. And it is hidden for this reason: that no one but he who is wise and learned can attain this art or bring it to **perfection**, performing all his tasks with knowledge and judgment. For if all men without knowledge and judgment could handle it—those who are driven only by a foolish audacity—they would have no more regard for the learning of the wise than they do for dogs, if they had been able to find or obtain the same through their own cleverness and intellect.

The fourth thing which you ought well to observe is the greatest secret of all, and mighty wisdom: that you know how to strengthen your **Medicine** <term>Medicine (Medicijne)</term><note>In alchemical texts, the "Medicine" is a common name for the Philosopher's Stone or Elixir, which was said to "heal" base metals into gold</note> and increase its powers; and this is a work of great prudence and wisdom. <header>THE MIRROR, etc. <note>original: "SPECULUM," Latin for "Mirror," referring to the title "Speculum Alchymiae" or "The Mirror of Alchemy."</note></header>
<meta>catchword: E</meta>

I believe that the *roots* <term>roots: likely referring to the "radical" or fundamental principles of alchemy, such as sulfur and mercury, or the primary materials used in the Great Work</term> have never been so gathered together as those that are here: understand this alone, and keep it in your memory, so as to draw wisdom from it, that you may hide it or reveal it when you see the opportunity for it, and as is proper. Do not show it to every frivolous person, who, through the looseness of his mind, would not value it; for then that which is precious would become contemptible. I shall give you an answer regarding all the aforementioned Articles, provided I maintain life and health, whether by word of mouth or through writing, so that when it pleases *God*, you shall understand the same well. And thus the true Mirror of *Alchemy* is ended.

> *One contains within itself Masculine and Feminine, therefore it is Hermaphroditic. Two contain Masculine, Feminine, and Spirit: three contain Body, Sun, and Moon.* <note>original Latin: "Unum continet in ſe Masculinum & Femininum, ergo Hermaphroditum. Duo continent Masculinum Femininum & Spiritum: tres Corpus Sol & Luna." This is a classic alchemical formula describing the progression from the unified "Rebis" or Hermaphrodite to the trinity of spirit, soul, and body, symbolized by the Sun (Gold) and Moon (Silver).</note>

->F I N I S.<-

<summary>This page marks the conclusion of the "Mirror of Alchemy," offering a final warning to the reader to keep these "roots" of knowledge secret from the unworthy. It concludes with a Latin alchemical aphorism regarding the union of masculine and feminine principles and the trinity of the Sun, Moon, and Body.</summary>
<keywords>Alchemy, secrecy, roots of the art, Hermaphrodite, Sun and Moon, Mirror of Alchemy, Speculum Alchymiae</keywords>

# THE TOMB OF SEMIRAMIS, <note>original Latin: "TUMBA SEMIRAMIDIS." Semiramis was a legendary Queen of Babylon. Legend says her tomb bore an inscription promising riches to any king who needed them; when Cyrus the Great opened it, he found only a rebuke for his greed. In alchemy, this serves as a metaphor for the "hidden" nature of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>
### *Sealed in the Hermetic Manner.* <note>The term "Hermetic" refers to Hermes Trismegistus, the mythical founder of alchemy. To "seal hermetically" originally meant to seal a glass vessel airtight for alchemical processes.</note>

Which, when it is opened by a wise person (and not by a domineering, greedy Cyrus), he shall find therein the treasures of the arts and inexhaustible riches to his satisfaction.

# TUMBA
<note>Latin: "Tomb." In this context, it refers to the "Tomb of Semiramis" mentioned on the previous page, a metaphor for the hidden secrets of alchemy.</note>

### CHAPTER II.

## What the Physical or Philosophical Gold is.

The *Philosophical Gold* or *Silver* is a metallic body, being dissolved into the final matter, namely into *Mercury*, which is the first matter of the **Stone**, and is thus proven; all things are made of that into which they are dissolved, and all metals are brought back again to *Argentum Vivum* <note>original: "Argentum Vivum." Latin for "living silver," the term used by alchemists for quicksilver or mercury.</note>: Therefore they were *Argentum Vivum* before.

For according to the common opinion of the Philosophers, it is in *Mercury* that which the *Wise* <note>The "Wise" or "Sages" are the historical masters of alchemy who have achieved the Great Work.</note> seek; moreover, *Mercury* is the *Radix* <term>Radix|Latin for "root." In alchemical theory, this refers to the foundational substance or "metallic seed" from which all metals originate.</term> in *Alchemy*, because from it, through it, and in it all metals exist. And *Theophrastus* <note>Theophrastus von Hohenheim, better known as Paracelsus (1493–1541), a revolutionary Swiss physician and alchemist who emphasized the chemical composition of the world.</note> (who

Aristotle <note>Medieval alchemists frequently attributed their own theories to Aristotle to lend them ancient authority, though these "pseudo-Aristotelian" texts focused on the transformation of matter.</note> mentions the weight, saying, *and cook until the earth (that is, the Gold) has dried out ten parts of the water.*

The *Author* of the *New Light* <note>original: *Novum Lumen Chymicum*, a highly influential work published in 1604 by the Polish alchemist Michael Sendivogius.</note> breaks out at the end of his books with these words: *There must be ten parts of water to one part of the Body; and by this means we make the Mercury without common mercury, by taking ten parts of our Mercurial water (that is, the Mercurial Oil or Salt, putrefied and distilled through an alembic <note>original: "gealembiceerd," referring to the use of a distilling apparatus called an alembic</note>), which is an oily vapor, to one part of the body of Gold. And being enclosed in a vessel, so through continuous boiling, the Gold is made into Mercury—that is, an oily vapor—and not common mercury, as some falsely imagine to themselves.*

->CHAPTER<- <note>original: "CAP.", abbreviation for "Capitulum" or Chapter.</note>

### CHAPTER V.

## What a Quintessence actually is.

Paracelsus <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), known as Paracelsus, was a Swiss physician and alchemist who pioneered the use of minerals in medicine and popularized the term "Quintessence."</note> in his third book regarding long life, Chapter 2, reasons thus: *A Quintessence is nothing other than the goodness of Nature, such that Nature enters into a spagyric <note>Spagyric refers to the alchemical process of separating a substance into its components and then reuniting them in a purified form; the term comes from the Greek words for "to pull" and "to gather."</note> mixture and moderation, in which nothing that is perishable or contradictory is found.*

And also in his fourth book *Archidoxis* <note>original: *Archidox.*, referring to the *Archidoxes of the Secrets of Nature*, one of Paracelsus’s foundational works on alchemical medicine.</note> he says: *A Quintessence <note>The "fifth essence," representing the pure, spiritual core of a substance, standing above the four classical elements of earth, air, fire, and water.</note> is a substance which is physically extracted from all growing things <note>original: "Crescitiven," derived from the Latin *crescere* (to grow), referring to plants or organisms that possess a life force.</note>, and from everything that has life, being separated from all impurity and mortality, purely refined, and divided from the elements thereof.*

And a little thereafter in the same place: *You must know regarding the Quintessence, that it is a substance small and slight, dwelling in some tree, herb, stone or suchlike,*

Rupescissa <note>Johannes de Rupescissa (c. 1310–1366) was a Franciscan friar and alchemist famous for his work on the "Quintessence," which he identified as the spiritual essence of matter, often extracted through the distillation of wine (alcohol).</note> says in the 5th Chapter toward the end, *Regarding the Quintessence: the Quintessence which we seek is therefore a thing made alive by Divine breathing; which is separated from the perishable body of the Four Elements through constant ascending and descending <note>This refers to the alchemical process of circulation or reflux, where a substance is repeatedly vaporized and condensed in a closed vessel to reach a higher state of purity.</note>: and the Reason is, because that which is sublimated <note>Sublimation in alchemy generally refers to the purification of a substance by heat, causing it to rise to the top of the vessel.</note> for a second time or more is subtler, more refined <note>original: "sterflijker," literally "more mortal." rosive, by which the *Quintessence* <term>Quintessence: the "fifth essence" or the purest, most medicinal part of a substance, separated from its physical "dross."</term> and the body are separated from one another. And in the third Chapter of *On Long Life* <note>original: *van het lang leven*; likely referring to the treatise *De Vita Longa* by Paracelsus.</note>, he says, in the sixth part and third Book: Dissolve *Gold* together with all the substance of *Gold*, by means of a Corrosive etc., and do this for so long that the same has become one with the Corrosive; and your desire must not be taken away by this, for a Corrosive is suitable for the gold, as the gold is, and without a Corrosive the gold is dead.

Yet you must nevertheless know that our *Menstruum*, when poured upon Gold, should not actually be called a Corrosive, but rather "fiery": as being the strength and power through which an *Arcanum* <term>Arcanum: a secret, powerful remedy or "great mystery" believed to have transformative healing properties.</term> overcomes all poison. For all *Quicksilver* <note>original: *Mercurius Vivus*</note>, *Sublimate*, and also *Precipitate* must die in the *Elixir of the Sun* <note>original: *Elixirium van Sol*; gold in its perfected, liquid medicinal form.</note>, and become a special and excellent *Tincture* <term>Tincture: a potent alchemical preparation used to "color" or transform base matter into a higher state.</term>; because a violent dissolution is not made by our *Menstruum*, as is done by those resurrected agents like *Strong Water* <note>original: *Aqua Fortis*; nitric acid, known for its ability to dissolve silver and other metals.</note> and *Royal Water* <note>original: *Aqua Regia*; a mixture of nitric and hydrochloric acids, the only common acid capable of dissolving gold.</note> and others of that nature,

<summary>This page explores the debate over whether the secret alchemical solvent is "corrosive" in the traditional sense. Citing Paracelsus, the author argues that while it dissolves gold, it is a "fiery" spiritual force rather than a violent acid like Aqua Regia.</summary>

### C H A P T E R  V I I I.

## The Practice of the Stone.

When you have obtained the Coloring Soul of the Planet <note>In alchemy, the "Planets" refer to the seven base and precious metals (e.g., Sol for Gold, Mars for Iron); the "Soul" is their concentrated essence or tincture.</note>, or its true *Quintessence* <term>Quintessence: the "fifth essence" or purest spiritual core of a substance, separated from its physical dross.</term>, through the preceding *putrefaction* <term>Putrefaction: the alchemical stage of breakdown or decay, necessary for the matter to be reborn and purified.</term>, in which the true *Mercury* and the true *Sulphur* of the Philosophers <note>These are not common mercury and sulfur, but the philosophical principles of fluidity/spirit and fixity/soul.</note> are contained, then your matter is suitably prepared to make our blessed *Stone* with it. Take therefore (in the Name of Him who spoke and all was there <note>A reference to the divine creation of the world through the Word.</note>) a suitable portion of this very pure matter, put it into a *Fixing-vessel* <term>Fixing-vessel: a specialized glass container, such as a "philosopher's egg," designed to withstand long periods of heat to "fix" a substance from a liquid or gas into a solid.</term> or Phial, or

# T U M B A

...[matter and place it] in a Philosophical *Egg* <term>Philosophical Egg: an oval-shaped glass vessel used to contain the alchemical matter during its transformation; it represents the "womb" where the Stone is nurtured.</term>, sealed in the Hermetic manner <note>meaning airtight, usually achieved by melting the glass neck of the vessel to fuse it shut.</note>, place it in an *Athanor* <term>Athanor: a specialized alchemical furnace designed to provide steady, consistent heat over long periods without constant supervision.</term>, as you know. Proceed with a suitable heat—that is to say, a digesting heat—continuously; for if this heat is lacking, the matter must necessarily die or become a miscarriage. This heat should be gentle, subtle, changing, and not burning (to use the *Count's* <note>Likely referring to Bernard of Trevisan (1406–1490), an Italian alchemist often referred to as "the Count," known for his long search for the Stone.</note> own words) from the first union until the perfect *Ablution* <term>Ablution: a stage of purification where the "impurities" are washed away from the matter, often associated with the appearance of a white color.</term>.

And regarding the governance of the fire, one may further consult the unknown *Philalethes* <note>Eirenaeus Philalethes was a famous 17th-century alchemist (often identified as George Starkey) whose writings focused heavily on the "regimen" or management of heat.</note>, who clearly describes the variety of the colors through the governance of each Planet <note>In this context, the "Planets" refer to the successive stages of the process, each ruled by a different metal/planet (e.g., the Regimen of Saturn, the Regimen of Jupiter).</note>. He describes coagulating and fixing these colors into the *white* or *red Stone*; for (as *Raymond Lullius* <note>Ramon Llull (c. 1232–1315), a philosopher to whom many foundational alchemical texts were attributed.</note> makes known) he who has no power or patience in the work shall spoil it through great haste.

### CHAPTER X.

# On the Physical use of the Stone, both internally and externally dissolved.

You must know that this blessed <term>Stone: The Philosopher's Stone, the legendary alchemical substance sought for both the transmutation of metals and the perfection of the human body.</term> is a general Universal Medicine; containing in itself the healing of all diseases, and as well in *hot* as *cold* <note>In historical medicine, diseases were classified by their "temperament" (hot, cold, wet, dry). A universal medicine was believed to balance these opposing states.</note>, so far as they are known by Nature to be curable, and are permitted by *God* to be healed. If you inquire how this most perfect Medicine and Heavenly *Tincture*, and such other general healings perform their work, healing contradictory things in the body of men; So we answer, that they accomplish all this through the warming, enlightening, and irradiation of the <term>Archeus: In the systems of Paracelsus and Van Helmont, the Archeus is the internal 'vital force' or 'spirit' that governs the body's health and functions.</term> Just as our Philosopher <term>Van Helmont: Jan Baptista van Helmont (1579–1644), a celebrated Flemish physician and chemist who bridged the gap between alchemy and modern medicine.</term> has named the same in a treatise: *There is in Herbs, Seeds, and Stones a great power, so that they certainly work without their dissolution.* <note>original: "Daar is in Kruyden, Zaaden, en Steenen een groote kragt, ſo dat ſy ſekerlijk werken ſonder haar ontbindinge."</note>

# The Wonderful EFFECTS,
### And the almost indescribable powers of the true and genuine *Oil*, which is made of
# NATIVE SULPHUR, <note>original: "Sulphur Vivum," referring to sulfur in its natural, "living" state as found in the earth.</note>
### Brought to *Fire*, and commonly named *Oil of Sulphur per Campanam*. <term>per Campanam: A distillation method where a glass bell (campana) is used to capture the vapors of burning sulfur, condensing them into a potent acid or "oil."</term>

To distinguish it from that deceitful *Oil of Sulphur*, which in place of this true *Oil* is unfaithfully prepared and sold by *Apothecaries* and *Druggists*, to the shame of the Art <note>In this context, "the Art" refers to Chymistry or Alchemy.</note> and the unspeakable harm of their deceived Patients.

# GEO. STARKEY,

sensitive to the great worry and burden he carried to provide for them while he lived; and he concluded that should he come to die, they would be forced to seek their bread from *door to door*. Therefore he came (says *Helmont*) to me, and desired something for the preservation of his life. I then (being a young man) pitied his condition and thought thus to myself: the smoke of burning *Sulfur* <note>original: "Sulphur"</note> has been found through experience to be powerful and effective in preserving wine from spoilage. Reflecting further on these thoughts, I concluded that the *acidic Liquor* <term>Liquor</term> or *Oil*, which is made from burning *Native Sulfur* <note>original: "Sulphur Vivum"</note>, necessarily retains the smoke within itself; indeed, the entire smell of the *Sulfur* is in truth nothing other than a *sulfurous* smoke, thoroughly saturated or "drowned" in its *Mercurial salt* <note>original: "Mercuriaal-zout"; in alchemy, this refers to a specific crystalline essence believed to be contained within minerals.</note>, and thus becomes a thickened *Liquor*. After this, I thought to myself: our blood being (for us) nothing other than the wine of our Life—and if that be preserved, then even if it does not lengthen life, it will at least preserve our health.

<summary>Helmont explains the logic behind his sulfur remedy, drawing an analogy between preserving wine with sulfur smoke and preserving human blood—the "wine of life"—to maintain health and longevity.</summary>

# GEO. STARKEY,

### *Postscript.*

In order that those who desire these so pleasant, effective, and advantageous Remedies may not be deceived by that worthless, counterfeit *Oil of Vitriol* <term>concentrated sulfuric acid derived from minerals</term>, which is called by the incorrect name *Oil of Sulfur* because it has been distilled once from common, unhealthy *Sulfur* and colored with the *barks of trees* or certain *roots*—with which the city and all *apothecary shops* are filled, to the great detriment of the <term>Art</term> <note>the "Art" refers to the Alchemical or Spagyric practice of medicine</note>, but much greater for those who use it in place of the true oil: which, in truth, does not possess a single quality of the same. ## My Diaphoretic, Diuretic, and Anodyne Elixir.

Its action is through sweat <term>diaphoretic: a medicine that induces perspiration to purge toxins</term> and urine <term>diuretic: a substance that promotes the production of urine</term>, softening pain <term>anodyne: a medicine used to relieve or soothe pain</term> and all violent disturbances in the body; it pacifies the *Archeus* <term>the internal vital spirit or governing life-force within the body, a key concept in the medical theories of Jan Baptist van Helmont</term>, and opens obstructions <note>internal blockages of vessels or organs, such as the liver or spleen, which were thought to cause disease</note> through a soothing quality, very powerful and pleasant. # GEORGE STARKEY’S

<margin>*A double Diaceltatesson, and first the Middle Way according to Helmont.*</margin>

This I call my common *Diaceltatesson*; it is exceptionally good for long-standing ailments, using 24 or 30 grains in two *Pills* every second or third day, and in between, using my sweat *Pill* in the evening, and in the morning, *Ens Veneris* <note>original: "Ens Veneris"; literally 'Essence of Venus,' a preparation Starkey famously made by distilling copper (or iron) with sal ammoniac.</note>. The patient may go outside in the afternoon if the weather is fair, but all excess must be avoided.

<margin>*The true Diaceltatesson or Corallatum of Paracelsus and Helmont.*</margin>
The other *Diaceltatesson* is my true *Corallatum* <term>Corallatum: A red-colored medicinal preparation, often a "red precipitate" of mercury or a specific antimonial compound, named for its coral-like hue.</term>, fundamentally curing *gout* <note>original: "sicht"; an archaic Dutch term for *jicht* (gout).</note>, the *Pox* <note>original: "Pokken"; in this context, it usually refers to syphilis, often called the "Great Pox."</note>, and all swellings in the *Throat*, *Lungs*, *Bladder*, and *Kidneys*; it also roots out all hot and cold Fevers, even if they are very long-standing, and where the *Pill* and *Ens Veneris* fall short. It heals, then, through a general purification, cleansing the vessels and fulfilling the desire of a careful Doctor, to the great strengthening of the patient; the *dose* is from 10 to 30 drops, in French Wine or White Beer.

# DR. STARKEY'S PILL.

Finding a precious pearl, which in the sight of all the world was cast onto a dung-heap (and the owner made no haste to claim it for himself), I could do no less than pick it up out of the filth and display its innate brilliance and purity, as much as was possible for me (who am but a young <term>practitioner</term><note>A junior physician or apothecary in training</note>).

### The Description of the Recipe by Mr. Matthews, given in the year 1659 to two of his friends.

Take two equal parts of *Indian Saltpeter* <note>Potassium nitrate, a key ingredient in early chemistry and gunpowder</note> and the best *Rhenish white Tartar* <note>Potassium bitartrate, or cream of tartar, sourced from the Rhine wine-producing regions</note>, crush them and sift them through a hair sieve: place them in a well-glazed pot, set it upon charcoal, then add the substances into it with a spoon all at once, until it is all burnt white, and this is called the *Salt of Tartar* <term>Salt of Tartar</term><note>Potassium carbonate; a chemical salt produced by heating cream of tartar. It was highly valued by alchemists as a "fixed" salt</note>: and if it will not burn, stir it with a glowing coal: and when it is burnt and while it is still warm, crush it into a coarse powder, and place it in a well-glazed dish, or what is even better, in a flat glass vessel, cover it to the breadth of two fingers with the *Oil of Turpentine*: stir it

### DR. STARKEY’S PILL.

Treasure of great value, when it is prepared correctly.

It consists of a fixed *Salt*, and of essential *Oils*, which through a proper *Fermentation* become a *Tertium Neutrum* <note>Latin: "a neutral third"; an alchemical term for a new substance created from two others that possesses the qualities of neither</note>, being distinct from both.

The *Salt* is named in the Recipe as the *Salt of Tartar* <term>Salt of Tartar: potassium carbonate, a salt produced by burning the sediment found in wine barrels; it was highly valued in alchemy for its purifying properties</term> because of its fine power: and because the *Pill* gains much credit from the name of *Mr. Matthews*, I shall use his testimony. In the latter part of his book called *The Unlearned Alchemist*, he uses this expression: If you (like a blind man) uproot the strong poison of these subjects, namely *poppies* <note>Original: "slaap-bollen" (sleep-balls), the seed pods of the opium poppy</note> and black *Hellebore*, then stop, you barking fools, and first learn to know the power of the *Salt of Tartar*. Do not make known your own folly and ignorance before you have experienced its powers, so that you cause no occasion for constant laughter among those whom experience has taught and instructed. *Salt seasons all things* <note>Original Latin: "Sal Sapit omnia"</note>; it makes all things savory; everyone knows its power to [render] things...
