### DR. STARKEY’S PILL.

to preserve [things] from decay: And among all fixed *Salts*, there is none so powerful as the *Salt of Tartar*, which *van Helmont* <note>Jan Baptista van Helmont (1580–1644), a pioneering chemist and physician who bridged the gap between alchemy and modern science</note> calls *its own Republic among Alkalis* <note>Original Latin: "Sua Inter Alcalia Res publica"</note>. *Dioscorides* <note>An ancient Greek physician and botanist</note> says the same is purifying, warming, binding, corrosive <note>Original: "eetende" (eating)</note>, and drying. It purifies the blood and cleanses the *Body*, says *Mullerus* <note>Likely referring to the chemist Philipp Müller</note>, in the most lamentable diseases; it makes itself into *Medicines* of excellent powers, according to its various methods and preparations. ### DR. STARKEY’S PILL.

It <note>Refers back to the pill described on the previous page.</note> is very powerful for dispelling wind <note>historical term for gas or flatulence</note>, it soothes the torments and gripings of the stomach and bowels, driving the winds away both upwards and downwards. The true cause of all griping (says my author) is an unnatural acidity that is harmful wherever it is found. Now, acidity has the spleen as its seat, and is the ferment of the stomach, by which digestion is completed; but if it is too acidic, it causes pains and wringing gripes, commonly called *stomach appetite* <note>original: *Orexis Stomachi*; referring here to a painful or excessive gnawing sensation in the stomach</note>. And whenever even the slightest acidity descends into the bowels or the mesenteric vessels <note>the veins and membranes that connect the intestines to the abdominal wall</note>, which has not been changed into a salty nature, it causes severe pains there, mixed with wind. For this, <term>Opium</term> <term>Opium: A potent extract of the poppy. In the "chymical" medicine of this era, it was highly valued for its ability to calm the body's internal systems when properly prepared.</term> is particularly good (when it is well-corrected <note>meaning the substance has been refined or purified to remove its "toxic" or overly sedative qualities</note>), giving both relief from those fermenting pains and extinguishing the malignant acidity that is the cause of them.

effective: although some Practitioners foolishly conclude that the *Medicine* is best which slightly disturbs Nature and works violently: For the power of a Medicine does not consist in its vomiting or Laxative qualities, which disturb Nature and agitate the *Archeus* <term>a term from Paracelsian medicine referring to the "internal chemist" or vital force that governs the body's functions</term>, causing uprisings and distress: But its excellence consists in its Friendly and pleasant service to Nature: through which it simultaneously refreshes and delights the vital spirits, and dissolves all supernatural <note>original: *boven natuurlijke*; in a medical context, this refers to "preternatural" or "morbid" substances that are not part of a healthy body</note> excrements and hardening.

It serves as praise for a good Medicine to heal not only *Safely & Quickly* <note>original: *Tuto & Cito*</note>, but also pleasantly; and then the Medicine works best when it is so prepared that a large *Dose* of it may be used with the least disturbances of the head and of the Stomach: which is the reason that some ignorant Persons despise it and set it aside as if it were devoid of power.

->*Recipe.*<-

Take the best *Tartar* <term>Cream of tartar; potassium bitartrate</term> and *Saltpeter* <term>potassium nitrate; a key ingredient in gunpowder and medicine</term>, powdered, and mixed one pound of each, or as much as you please: put the ingredients into a clean iron Kettle, ignite them with a coal, and they shall burn with a great noise, and after the flame is over, stir the Mass diligently with an iron Rod, until the redness disappears, and the Mass becomes a very white *Salt*, possessing both natures, of the *Tartar* and *Salniter* <note>original: "Salniter," a synonym for saltpeter</note>: which is a noble Salt, and produces an active *Alkali*.

But if you would rather desire the *Alkali* from the *Tartar* alone, then take as much as you please, and send it to a Glass-house or Pottery to calcine <note>to heat a substance to a high temperature to remove volatile parts or change its structure</note>, and it will become a white Salt-cake.

But I gave (established in my *Pyrotechnia* <note>George Starkey’s famous work, *Pyrotechnia: or, The Compleat Chymical Philosopher*, published in 1658</note>) Reasons, why the *Tartar* which is not calcined with *Saltpeter*, is not less and in some cases much better, than the Salt

# DR. STARKEY’S PILL.

The prescribed manner for purifying the Salt is worthy of your serious consideration; The *Tartar* <term>Salt of Tartar; potassium carbonate derived from the sediment of wine casks</term> cannot be made into such a pure Salt by burning: for there will always still be some impurity present, which is removed by this method.

The Art and the manner of making this composition is of particular importance. The *Opium* (of which that which comes from the Strait <note>original: "uyt de Straat"; likely referring to the Levant or the Strait of Gibraltar, common trade routes for high-quality "Turkey opium" at the time</note> is the best) cannot be purified without dissolution, through which its powers are not diminished: and when the dissolution is performed with Spirit of Wine <term>distilled alcohol/ethanol</term>, it helps to improve the same.

The various moistenings and dryings of the *Pill* are of very good use: for by this means the Sulfur-like <note>In 17th-century Paracelsian chemistry, "sulfur" referred to the oily, flammable, and often narcotic or "volatile" principle of a substance</note> part of the *Opium*, in which its harmfulness resides, is greatly reduced and entirely subdued.

# ->DR. STARKEY’S PILL.<-

And now whoever you may be, who undertakes the making of this *Pill*, see that you are very cautious, for you deal with dangerous objects; and it is no work for a **Raw** and ignorant **Youth** <note>In the 17th century, the "art" of chemistry was viewed as a serious discipline requiring moral maturity and long experience; an "ignorant youth" was someone lacking the specialized training to handle potent substances safely</note>.

Two or three things were greatly feared in the general preparation of this **Recipe**: the first, that the making of the *Pill* might be undertaken by ignorant **Persons** who lack judgment, or those who are careless and hasty, who without any prior caution would mix these things together before they have come to their maturity and have been relieved of their malignancy <note>original: "quaadaardigheyd"; in early modern pharmacy, this referred to the "crude" or toxic nature of raw ingredients that needed to be "tamed" or purified through chemical processes</note>, and are then dangerous.

The second thing which was feared is that this might fall into the hands of profit-seeking people who consider their own advantage more than the usefulness to others; and then this **Medicine** would be corrupted, when they would take spoiled materials for it that have lost their potency or are adulterated, like the *Opium* that comes from the *Indies* <note>While "Strait" or Turkish opium was prized for its quality, opium imported from the East Indies was frequently suspected of being diluted or "adulterated" with other gums or resins by traders</note> which is much subject [to this]: as is said by

And truly, this is the boogeyman <note>original: *bulle-bak*; a folkloric monster or bugbear used to scare children, here used to describe a medical scare tactic</note> that the *Doctors* use to scare people away from using it. "Watch what you are doing" (they say), "for there is *Opium* in it, and *Opium* remains *Opium*, even after everything has been done to it that can be done"; and yet this *Opium* is the primary ingredient of their greatest Medicines, as I have pointed out before. And to use Mr. *Parkinson's* <note>John Parkinson (1567–1650), a renowned English apothecary and herbalist to King Charles I</note> own words, it is a very honored ingredient in these great compositions of *Theriac* <term>Theriac: a famous ancient "universal" cure-all and antidote consisting of dozens of ingredients, often including opium</term>, *Mithridate* <term>Mithridate: a complex semi-mythical antidote against poisons, highly valued in early modern medicine</term>, etc.; and the same can be said of *Hellebore* <note>Helleborus; a plant used as a potent purgative, feared for its toxicity if not properly prepared</note>.

<meta>catchword: Pof</meta>

<summary>This page addresses the medical establishment's use of "Opium" and "Hellebore" as scare tactics to discourage patients from using certain remedies, despite these same substances being core ingredients in prestigious medicines like Theriac and Mithridate.</summary>

<keywords>Opium, Hellebore, medical scare tactics, John Parkinson, Theriac, Mithridate, George Starkey, early modern pharmacy</keywords>

--- Page 327 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the general discussion of Opium and Hellebore in the previous page to a personal statement by George Starkey regarding the authorship and improvement of his famous medical pill.</meta>

<page-num>259</page-num>

### ->DR. STARKEY’S PILL.<-

<meta>catchword: he</meta>

## Postscript.
<note>original: *Poft Script*</note>

*Friendly Reader.*

Having now been acquainted for a full year with *Mr. George Kendal*, the author of this preceding treatise, and having been informed by him of the instructions which he had received from the **Unlearned Alchemist** <note>original: *Ongeleerden Alchimift*; likely a reference to a practitioner without formal academic training who shared a crude version of the recipe</note> to prepare the *Diaphoretic* <term>Diaphoretic: a medicine used to induce sweating to break a fever</term> and *Diuretic Pill* <term>Diuretic: a substance that promotes the production of urine</term>, I had the opportunity to investigate his preparation and the manner in which it was handled. I instructed him in some particulars on how he could make the *Pill* ten times better with little effort and cost; and furthermore, how to elevate that same process far beyond what *Mr. Matthews* <note>Richard Matthews, who became famous for "Matthews’s Pill," which Starkey claimed was a stolen and inferior version of his own formula</note> ever knew or did. According to these instructions, he has made his Pill, surpassing that of the **Unlearned Alchemist** in strength as far as a *Diamond* exceeds a *Brick* in value, as those who desire it may find for themselves. I may judge myself capable of distinguishing this, as I am the first author and inventor thereof, who is ever known through work or writings in *Europe*, indeed in the entire world; and it was from me that *Mr. Matthews* received what he had, though for another purpose than he...

<summary>George Starkey asserts his status as the original inventor of a powerful medicinal pill, claiming he has taught George Kendal how to vastly improve the recipe beyond the versions used by others like Richard Matthews.</summary>

<keywords>George Starkey, George Kendal, Richard Matthews, alchemical medicine, Diaphoretic Pill, Diuretic Pill, medical authorship, 17th-century pharmacy</keywords>

--- Page 328 ---
<meta>This page concludes the postscript by George Starkey, continuing from the previous page where he defends his claim as the original inventor of the "Pill" against the misappropriation by Mr. Matthews and the "Unlearned Alchemist."</meta>

<page-num>260</page-num>

<header>DR. STARKEY’S PILL.</header>

<margin>25th</margin> he used the same. Many learned and honest people know that this secret was known and used by me in the year 1651, five years before his name became known to the world through this. To what extent this preparation has been improved by me, I shall explain at length in a treatise which I intend to publish shortly: in which I shall give a full explanation and make a desirable discovery of such medicinal secrets <note>original: *Medicinale verborgentheden*</note>, compared to which this shall be considered but a trifle, although it surpasses all Galenical compositions <term>Galenical compositions: traditional medicines made from plant or animal materials, following the teachings of the ancient physician Galen, often contrasted with the "chemical" medicines of the alchemists</term>. I have thought it well to add this here for the honor and defense of this learned and truly sincere Author <note>Referring to Mr. George Kendall, mentioned on the previous page</note>. As a testimony and vindication for him, to rescue him from that mist which sought to stifle his reputation; which had arisen from an envious dung-hook, seeking to find a precious Pearl on a rubbish heap; which is claimed by

London 19 October ->GEORGE STARKEY:<-
1663. ->*Being a Philosopher by Fire.*<-
<note>original: *Een Philoſooph ſijnde door het Vuur*; a translation of the Latin *Philosophus per Ignem*, a title used by alchemists to signify their expertise in chemical transformation through heat.</note>
